- claudie-pierlot (Brand, enriched) — French women's fashion brand founded by designer Claude Anne Marie Pierlot (born Troyes 1947, died Nanterre Feb 2009); career: knitwear factories → Dorothée Bis → Printemps design studio → Mafia agency → René Derhy (1974); launched own label mid-1980s from Derhy premises (6 rue du Faubourg-Poissonnière); mid-1990s moved to rue du Sentier; 3 Paris boutiques including historic flagship 23 rue du Vieux-Colombier; known for "dressing the little women of Paris" (WWD 1992); "Parisian, feminine and urban" brand identity; sold to SMCP shareholders (Sandro/Maje) early 2009; SMCP group formed for acquisition; died February 2009; 2010 Audrey Marnay advertising muse + capsule; L Capital (LVMH-linked, 41%) + Florac (Louis-Dreyfus) 2010–2013 SMCP stakes; YAML market_categories includes "Menswear" and "Skateboarding" (pipeline artefacts, preserved); SPD relevance: Parisian feminine seasonal print and textile vocabulary in accessible luxury womenswear

- babochka (Brand) — Russian luxury multi-brand boutique chain; HQ St. Petersburg; founded 1988 by Boris Avsadzhanashvili as first private boutique in Leningrad (Furmanova/Gagarinskaya Street); managed by Khatulya Avsadzhanashvili (BoF 500); boutiques tripled under her leadership; 200+ designers/brands represented; direct partnerships: Chanel, Louis Vuitton, Dior, Fendi; boutiques under Babochka's auspices: Valentino, Loro Piana, Ermenegildo Zegna, Berluti; Italian Alta Gamma award; online store babochka.ru (personal stylists, all-Russia fittings, weekly editorial content); SPD relevance: curatorial function distributes print-heavy luxury fashion houses (Fendi double-F monogram, Dior florals, LV monogram canvas) to Russian luxury market; no proprietary design function
- barneys-new-york (Brand) — American luxury fashion brand; founded 1923 Barney Pressman (500 sq ft, 7th Ave/17th St, men's discount); transformed to luxury by Fred Pressman 1960s–70s; introduced Giorgio Armani (1976), Comme des Garçons, Azzedine Alaïa, Christian Louboutin, Zegna to American market; International House 70,000 sq ft expansion 1970; women's added 1976; CO-OP contemporary sub-format 1986 (spun off to 20+ standalone stores, retired 2013); Isetan joint venture 1989; first Tokyo Barneys November 1990; Madison Avenue flagship 1997 (Kohn Pedersen Fox, 230,000 sq ft, $267M); Chapter 11 January 1996 (Isetan dispute); Jones Apparel Group → Istithmar PJSC $937.4M (2007); Perry Capital 2012 (debt $590M → $50M); Mark Lee CEO 2010, Daniella Vitale CEO 2017; Chapter 11 August 2019; ABG acquired IP $271.4M October 2019; Barneys at Saks opened January 2021 (54,000 sq ft 5th floor); both US Barneys at Saks locations closed early 2026 (Saks Global bankruptcy); Japan: 5 full-line + 4 outlet stores (Laox Holdings from April 2023); Barneys Beauty Seoul (Gloent Group licence); Tulum boutique hotel late 2024; ABG exploring Madison Avenue revival, Naples FL store (2027); Simon Doonan creative director 1988–2010 (window display as retail surface design practice); Spring 2011 campaign by Klein/Goldin/Teller; SPD relevance: historically most influential US curator of design-forward international fashion; introduced major fashion surface design practitioners to American market; window display practice as systematic retail visual design; Barneys Beauty = packaging/brand surface design product launch
- bebe-stores (Brand) — American women's contemporary fashion brand; established 1976 Manny Mashouf (Polk Street San Francisco); IPO 1998 (née "Babe, Inc."); peak 312 stores (215 core, 32 2b Bebe outlet, 64 BebeSport, 1 accessories); US/Canada + 15 international markets; BebeSport launched 2003, converted to PH8 November 2009; 2b Bebe = clearance + logo merchandise; bridal line 2012 (Rami Kashou, Project Runway runner-up), withdrawn for poor sales; Emilia Fabricant president 2010–2012; closed all 168–175 brick-and-mortar stores April 2017; Global Brands Group partnership June 2017; NYC lifestyle concept store March 2018; Centric Brands (licensee) Chapter 11 May 2020; celebrity campaigns: Mischa Barton, Rebecca Romijn, Eva Longoria, Bebe Rexha; 2009 spring campaign (Camilla Åkrans, Julia von Boehm); SPD relevance: in-house design function for women's contemporary fashion ("sexy contemporary" market = embellishment, sequins, metallic, bold prints, bodycon surface decoration); logo merchandise at outlet (branded surface motif); BebeSport/PH8 colour-block graphic sportswear; fragrance packaging design
- benetton-group (Brand) — Italian global fashion company; HQ Ponzano Veneto; founded 1965 Belluno (Veneto); first store, Paris boutique 1968; ~5,000 stores (franchise model); wholly owned by Edizione family holding (delisted 2012); secondary brand Sisley; iconic 1980s–1990s (Interbrand 75th 2000, off list 2002); €180M loss 2017, €100M loss 2024; 418+ store closures announced 2024–2025; Luciano Benetton returned aged 83 (2017); Jean-Charles de Castelbajac as artistic director; Oliviero Toscani re-appointed (died January 13, 2025); Massimo Renon CEO from 2020; Claudio Sforza CEO 2024; Devbhumi collaboration 2017 (scarves, 6,000 rural Indian women artisans, Uttarakhand); IWTO congress host 2019; Formula One: Tyrrell sponsor 1983, Benetton Formula 1985, Schumacher championships 1994+1995, sold Renault $120M 2000; Rana Plaza 2013 (Benetton clothing found in rubble); SPD relevance: "United Colors of Benetton" = colour-as-brand-identity = colour selection/coordination is the primary SPD output; Toscani billboard campaigns = photography as surface design at architectural scale; Jean-Charles de Castelbajac = graphic/colour-saturated fashion as creative direction; Devbhumi scarves = handcraft textile surface design collaboration documented; IWTO engagement = wool textile industry connection; one of the clearest examples of colour strategy as complete commercial identity
- bestseller (Brand) — Danish privately held family fashion company; founded 1975 Troels Holch Povlsen in Brande Denmark; CEO Anders Holch Povlsen (son); ~17,000 employees; 70+ markets; ~9,000 shops (6,000 China); women's: ONLY (largest brand), Vero Moda (Gisele Bündchen 2007–08), Mamalicious, VILA, SELECTED Femme, Junarose, Y.A.S, Noisy May, PIECES, Object, Jacqueline de Yong, produkt, JJXX, Topshop JV w/ASOS; menswear: Jack & Jones (denim origin 1990, 1st store Trondheim 1991, 1,000 stores 2025, Haas F1 + Astralis sponsor), Selected Homme, LMTD/Outfitters Nation, Only & Sons, Topman JV w/ASOS; children's: Name It; other: Normal variety chain (71.29%); Heartland (parent holding): 29.5% ASOS, 10% Zalando, $300M About You (2018); Bestseller Tower (Dorte Mandrup, 320m, announced 2017, shelved 2020); supply chain: Cambodia "slave-like" conditions (DanWatch/Guardian 2017), Uyghur labour (Dec 2023 report); SPD relevance: multi-brand European fast fashion group running numerous parallel design teams per season; Jack & Jones = denim surface treatment heritage (wash, distress, embellishment); ONLY + Vero Moda = high-volume women's seasonal print production at European scale; Topshop JV = access to UK's most print-forward high street brand heritage; Selected brands = contemporary tailoring fabric selection (stripe, check, houndstooth); one of Europe's largest privately held fashion design operations

- aritzia (Brand) — Canadian "everyday luxury" fashion retailer; founded 1984 Vancouver BC by Brian Hill; TSX IPO October 2016; CEO Jennifer Wong (from May 2022); Reigning Champ acquisition $63M (June 2021, menswear/athleisure); Fred Segal brand acquisition announced February 2026; 101 North American stores as of January 2021 (68 Canada, 44 US, incl. 13,000 sq ft Manhattan flagship and Chicago Gold Coast); multi-brand in-house portfolio: Wilfred, Wilfred Free, TNA, Talula, Babaton, Golden, Sunday Best, Main Character, Community, Super Puff (and others); handbag lines: SIXELEVEN + Auxiliary (2014); also carries Citizens of Humanity, Mackage, New Balance, Levi's, rag & bone, Adidas, Herschel, Havaianas, J Brand; e-commerce + digital magazine from November 2012; store interiors designed individually by architects per location (natural materials, custom furniture, art, no mirrors in most dressing rooms); Berkshire Partners exited 2019; SPD relevance: active multi-brand in-house design function (separate aesthetics per sub-brand); bespoke store interior design investment; "everyday luxury" positioning requires design quality signal above price point; Super Puff as signature designed product; Fred Segal addition brings LA design culture icon
- asics (Brand) — Japanese multinational sportswear company; HQ Kobe, Japan; founded January 1, 1949 as Onitsuka Co. (basketball shoes); ASICS Corporation name from 1977 (merger with GTO + Jelenk); net sales ¥570.4B FY2023, net income ¥35.2B; revenue split: 50% performance running shoes, 33% other shoes, 6% apparel/equipment, 11% Onitsuka Tiger; geographic: 21% North America, 27% Europe, 14% China, 16% Japan, 21% other; Haglöfs acquired 2010 (¥11.4B), sold LionRock Capital December 2023; Runkeeper app acquired February 2016; Unoha women's lifestyle brand (March 2021–June 2024); original logo typeface designed by Herb Lubalin (1977–2006, retained on Asics Tiger line); Asics Tiger lifestyle brand launched 2015 (1970s–1990s archive revival, designed by Bruce Mau Design); sponsorships: World Athletics, LA Marathon, Japanese Olympic Committee, Cricket Australia, Novak Djokovic; Nike origin connection: Phil Knight founded Blue Ribbon Sports to sell Onitsuka Tiger in US (1963), relationship ended in litigation; SPD relevance: Tiger Stripes (Mexico 66) = one of the most recognised footwear surface motifs globally; Onitsuka Tiger lifestyle brand = stripe motif as commercial heritage asset; Asics Tiger = archival graphic design revival strategy; Herb Lubalin typography preserved as brand surface asset; performance compression/swimwear = technical textile surface; national team uniform commissions
- authentic-brands-group (Brand) — American brand IP licensing platform; HQ NYC; founded 2010 by Jamie Salter; CEO Matt Maddox (from May 2026), Executive Chairman Salter; 50+ consumer brands; ~$38B total annual retail sales across portfolio (post-Guess); business model = acquire IP, license to operating partners, no direct manufacturing/retail; founding capital $250M from Leonard Green & Partners; valued $12.7B (November 2021, CVC/HPS deal); key acquisitions: Silver Star + Tapout (2010), Marilyn Monroe likeness (Jan 2011), Juicy Couture $195M (2013), Ali estate + Elvis Presley Enterprises (2013), Nautica from VF Corp (2018), Nine West (2018), Camuto Group with DSW (2018), Thomasville/Henredon/Drexel home brands (2018), Sports Illustrated $110M (2019), Volcom from Kering (2019), Barneys New York (2019), Brooks Brothers with Simon Property Group (2020), Forever 21 (2020), Reebok from Adidas $2.5B+ (2021/2022), PVH brands Izod/Van Heusen/Arrow/Geoffrey Beene $220M (2021), Eddie Bauer (2021), Ted Baker (2022), David Beckham brand partnership (2022), Boardriders $1.25B = Quiksilver/Billabong/Roxy/RVCA/DC Shoes/Element/Von Zipper (2023), Sperry (2024, licensed to Aldo Group), Champion from Hanesbrands $1.2B (2024), Dockers from Levi Strauss $311M (2025), Guess majority stake ~$1.4B valuation (January 2026), Kevin Hart brand (2026), Lee Jeans $750M from Kontoor (announced May 2026); Sports Illustrated: $110M (2019), Arena Group licence terminated January 2024, relicensed Minute Media March 2024; Authentic Live events division (launched 2025); SPD relevance: one of world's largest holders of fashion brand IP; - valentino (Brand) — Italian luxury fashion house; founded 1960 Rome by Valentino Garavani on Via Condotti; HQ/creative base Palazzo Gabrielli-Mignanelli Rome; revenue €1.31B (2024); Kering 30% stake (July 2023, full acquisition intended by 2028); Mayhoola majority ownership since 2012 (€700M); prior ownership chain: HdP → Marzotto → Permira; CDs: Garavani (1959–2007), Facchinetti (2007–2008), Chiuri+Piccioli (2008–2016), Piccioli sole (2016–2024), Alessandro Michele (2024–present); CEO Ricardo Bellini (from 2025); 1967 "no colour" collection = deliberate rejection of psychedelic patterns; V logo 1967; Rockstud accessory line (Chiuri/Piccioli era) = stud-grid surface embellishment; Red Valentino diffusion line discontinued Fall/Winter 2023; fragrance licensed L'Oréal (from 2018), eyewear Akoni Group (10-year from 2021); SS2016 African motifs/prints collection — cultural insensitivity criticism; Valentino Bags Lab court administration (May 2025, worker abuse); SPD relevance: pattern as explicit aesthetic decision from 1967; African cultural prints controversy; Rockstud stud grid as repeating surface decoration; Michele's maximal pattern-rich aesthetic signals design direction shift
- veepee (Marketplace) — French online flash sales platform; formerly vente-privee.com (founded 2001 by Jacques-Antoine Granjon + 7 partners); concept: limited-time online sales of designer brand overstock at 50–70% discount without harming brand image; Summit Partners 20% stake (July 2007); US joint venture with American Express (launched 2011, closed 2014); European expansion via acquisitions: vente-exclusive (Benelux), Privalia (Spain/Italy/Brazil/Mexico), Eboutic.ch (Switzerland), Designers&Friends (Denmark), ZloteWyprzedaze (Poland); rebranded Veepee January 2019 (10 European countries); theatre portfolio diversification (3 Paris theatres from 2014); panda-ticket.com (June 2017); €80M R&D investment (2017); Veepee Impulse accelerator at Station F (Fashion/Tech/Retail); SPD relevance: indirect — flash sales distribution channel for end-of-season designer fashion (including patterned/printed product); brand image protection model explicitly treats design identity as commercial asset; Station F accelerator in Paris fashion tech ecosystem; no in-house design function
- vera-bradley (Brand) — American accessories and travel goods brand; founded 1982 Fort Wayne Indiana by Barbara Bradley Baekgaard and Patricia R. Miller ($500 borrowed capital); named after Baekgaard's mother; HQ Roanoke Indiana (2026); CEO Ian Bickley (March 2026); founding concept: bright patterned women's bags vs. dull airport carry-ons; DEFINING FEATURE: entire brand identity built on surface pattern (florals, paisleys, geometric prints, elongated diamond quilting); pattern inspiration: Pierre Deux French Provençal look; limited seasonal pattern releases + retired pattern archive; paisley documented as consistent pattern "since the company's creation"; licensing of Vera Bradley designs to third-party manufacturers; production moved overseas ~2007, China reduced from ~95% to under 50% by 2019 (Myanmar, Cambodia, Vietnam); Spring Outlet Sales sold out 2005 (per-person cap 2006); Pura Vida Bracelets 75% stake (2019); Vera Bradley Foundation for Breast Cancer (1998) + special pink awareness fabric patterns; SPD relevance: among the most directly SPD-centred commercial brands in the dataset — pattern IS the product; seasonal pattern collections; retired pattern archive; pattern licensing; French Provençal heritage; quilting as structural surface design
- vera-wang (Brand) — American fashion designer; born 1949 NYC; figure skating background (1968 US Championships); Vogue editor 17 years (youngest ever); Ralph Lauren 2 years; independent bridal design from age 40; opened first salon Carlyle Hotel NYC 1990; bridal boutiques NYC/London/Tokyo/Sydney; first Asia flagship Cheongdam-dong Seoul (June 2012); CFDA Womenswear Designer of the Year 2005; CFDA Lifetime Achievement 2013; revenues ~$630M (Forbes 2018); brand IP sold WHP Global December 2024 (Wang continues as Founder/CCO); celebrity wedding gowns: Chelsea Clinton, Ivanka Trump, Alicia Keys, Mariah Carey, Victoria Beckham, Kim + Khloe Kardashian; figure skating costumes: Kerrigan, Kwan, Lysacek, Nathan Chen; US Figure Skating Hall of Fame 2009; Vera Wang China + Crystal Collection (home goods, 2002); Simply Vera diffusion at Kohl's (2007); White by Vera Wang at David's Bridal (2011, $600–$1,400); Black by Vera Wang at Men's Wearhouse (2012); SPD relevance: bridal gown as high-craft textile surface design (embroidered lace, beading, embellishment); figure skating costumes = performance garment surface engineering; home tableware collection = ceramic/crystal surface design; Simply Vera at Kohl's = mass-market RTW print/pattern at scale
- zalando (Marketplace) — German publicly traded online fashion retailer; HQ Berlin; founded 2008 by Robert Gentz + David Schneider (Samwer brothers capital); name from Spanish "zapatos"; initially footwear only; apparel 2010; beauty 2018; Frankfurt Stock Exchange 2014; DAX component September 2021; 51M+ active users in 25 European markets; revenue €10.5B (2024); ~16,000 employees; Partner Program (brands sell via platform own inventory/fulfilment); Lounge by Zalando flash sales; 12 German outlet stores; beauty concept store Berlin; Bread & Butter trade fair acquired June 2015 (discontinued 2018); Kickz acquisition March 2017; Anatwine 20% stake (2015, Cheltenham, retail integration software); About You acquisition €1.13B (announced Dec 2024, closed July 2025, full subsidiary Nov 2025); Vestiaire Collective luxury resale partnership (May 2026); Erfurt fulfilment centre closure Jan 2026 (~2,700 employees); ESG: 78% Scope 1+2 emissions reduction by 2023; labour controversies ZDF (2012), RTL (2014); SPD relevance: Europe's largest online fashion platform and primary digital distribution channel for pattern/print-forward fashion across 25 markets; own-brand design function; Partner Program embeds the platform into every European fashion brand's commercial infrastructure; Lounge by Zalando = flash sales for seasonal printed fashion
- zumiez (Retailer) — American specialty action sports retailer; founded 1978 Seattle by Thomas Campion + Gary Haakenson as "Above the Belt" (Northgate Mall); renamed Zumiez 1980s; HQ Lynnwood Washington; CEO Richard Brooks; publicly traded 2005; 726 stores (Feb 2018): 608 US, 52 Canada, 54 Europe, 12 Australia; focus: skateboarding, snowboarding, BMX, motocross apparel/footwear/accessories/hardgoods for youth demographic; acquisitions: Fast Forward (2006), Blue Tomato (European action sports, 2012), Fast Times (Australia, 2015); Couch Tour 13-year event (skate demos, live music, amateur comp; 2013 sponsors: Deathwish, DGK, Neff, Oakley, Adidas, Bones Bearings); Zumiez Foundation (2002, 180,000+ items to 180+ orgs 2012); SPD relevance: action sports retail = graphically intensive commercial fashion category; skateboard/snowboard culture = bold illustrative graphic tradition on apparel surfaces; sublimated snowboard outerwear; screen-printed graphic tees; branded embroidery/woven patches on accessories; Deathwish + DGK = graphic-identity-forward action sports brands; Blue Tomato extends channel internationally

- tous (Brand) — Spanish jewellery, accessories, and fashion retailer; founded Catalonia 1920 (watch repair workshop, Salvador Tous Blavi + Teresa Ponsa Mas); second generation: son Salvador + Rosa Oriol (married 1965) redirected to jewellery design; 400+ stores in 45 countries (NYC, Paris, Madrid, Moscow, Milan, Tokyo); 30 annual collections (as of 2012); product range: jewellery, bags, watches, perfumes, eyeglasses, textiles, small leather goods; national expansion 1980s; daughters Rosa, Alba, Laura, Marta joined family business; international from 1990s (Japan first); celebrity ambassadors: Kylie Minogue, Jennifer Lopez (SS 2011); Eugenia Martínez de Irujo collabs (Duchess of Montoro: "Iluminada," "Leyendas," "Cercle"); Manolo Blahnik for TOUS (2011, pendants reproducing "Campari" shoe in gold/silver); (RED) campaign partner (2012); estimated sales €450M total, €30M watches (2010/2011); SPD relevance: TOUS bear as recurring brand motif across all product categories; textiles explicitly in product range; bags/leather goods surface design; 30-collection/year design cadence; jewellery as surface embellishment
- true-religion (Brand) — American premium denim company; established 2002 Vernon CA by Jeff Lubell + Kym Gold; launched with unusual model: produced 14,000 pairs jeans before any orders; flagship store 2005 Manhattan Beach CA; sold in 900 boutiques + specialty stores in 50 countries on six continents (2009); distribution: Von Maur, Nordstrom, Bloomingdale's, Saks Fifth Avenue; NASDAQ TRLG; acquired TowerBrook Capital Partners May 2013 ($835M, 152% premium); Chapter 11 bankruptcy July 2017 ($534.7M liabilities, $243.3M assets, 27 store closures); second Chapter 11 April 2020 (37 closures, 49 stores remaining); completed November 2020; rebound 2021; CEO Michael Buckley (Nov 2019); creative director Zihaad Wells (re-hired 2019, had previously left Levi's Europe 2006 to join True Religion); SPD relevance: horseshoe back-pocket embroidered stitch = signature brand surface motif; decorative stitching and embellishment on denim as primary brand differentiation; contrast thread colour on indigo denim; logo embroidery; widely imitated motif = measure of commercial surface design value

- urban-outfitters (Retailer) — Multinational lifestyle retail corporation; HQ Philadelphia PA; founded 1970 as Free People (Richard Hayne, Judy Wicks, Scott Belair, UPenn entrepreneurship class); renamed Urban Outfitters 1976; portfolio: Urban Outfitters, Anthropologie, Free People, Terrain, BHLDN, Vetri Family restaurant group; private label wholesale design division produces much of merchandise; National Preservation Honor Award 2007 (Naval Shipyard campus); Q4 2015 acquired Vetri Family restaurant group; Nuuly rental (launched 2019); Nuuly Thrift (resale platform); Fashion Transparency Index 11–20% (2021); no disclosed factory supply chain; cultural controversies: Navajo Nation (trademarked name on products), ADL (offensive imagery), NAACP, keffiyeh "anti-war woven scarf" (2007); SPD relevance: private label wholesale division = active internal surface pattern + print design operation; housewares category (bedding, textiles, ceramics) = direct SPD procurement at commercial scale; Anthropologie sub-brand = one of US retail's most pattern-forward brands (botanical, floral, nature-motif prints on clothing + home); Free People = boho print, batik-referencing, artisanal surface treatments; BHLDN bridal textiles; cultural pattern controversies illustrate commercial stakes of SPD origin attribution

- takko (Retailer) — European discount fashion retailer; founded 1982; ~2,000 stores in 17 European countries; ~18,000 employees; full family range (baby, children, women, men) under private label brands; "Smart Discounter" positioning; omnichannel; SPD relevance: high-volume seasonal print and graphic rotation at value price points across family clothing; private label means all pattern/print decisions internal or own-label; kidswear, swimwear, lingerie all involve specific surface design briefs at high unit volumes; no design function, director, or supplier relationships documented in source material
- the-realreal (Marketplace) — American authenticated luxury resale marketplace; founded 2011 by Julie Wainwright; 38M+ members; ~40M items sold (as of Sept 2023); IPO Nasdaq June 2019 (REAL), raised $300M; $288M VC raised by July 2018; current CEO Rati Sahi Levesque (appointed Oct 2024); full-year profitability end of 2024; 17 Luxury Consignment Offices (14 browsable); first retail store NYC 2017; AI/ML pricing + human authentication; Stella McCartney sustainability partnership (2018); 4.68B litres water use avoided; 85,857 metric tonnes GHG avoided; counterfeit controversies: Chanel lawsuit (2018), Forbes investigation $3,600 fake Dior bag (2019), class action (2020); SPD relevance: indirect — largest US authenticated luxury resale platform circulating printed/patterned luxury fashion at scale; authentication requires brand-specific pattern recognition; no in-house design function
- thredup (Marketplace) — American online resale platform; founded 2009 by James Reinhart, Chris Homer, Oliver Lubin; HQ Oakland CA; Nasdaq-listed 2021 (TDUP); raised $131M; 137M+ secondhand items processed from ~55,000 brands; H1 2023: sales €99.8M (+4%), loss reduced to €38.5M; Clean Out Kit B2C model; B2B Resale as a Service (RaaS) — brand retailers give Clean Out Kits to customers for own store credit; Thrift Cards (2019); distribution centres US + Europe; SPD relevance: indirect — mass-market secondhand fashion platform distributing printed/patterned garments at scale from 55,000+ brands; RaaS embeds resale infrastructure into fashion brand retail ops; circular economy positioning; no in-house design function

- pepe-jeans (Brand) — Denim and casual wear brand; founded 1973 Portobello Road Market London by three brothers; HQ Sant Feliu de Llobregat, Spain; owned by M1 Group (Beirut) since Feb 2015, acquired alongside Hackett London; also owns Norton Clothing (2015, Norton Motorcycle tribute) and Façonnable (French brand, 2016); CEO Marcella Wartenbergh from Sept 2019; former CEO Carlos Ortega retains 20%+; global ambassadors: Dua Lipa (2019), Lila Moss (2023, 50th anniversary); India: ~200 stores + 100+ planned over 3 years; Red Bull Racing sponsor since 2010; 2024: title partner Red Bull Racing Pepe Jeans Academy Programme (F1 Academy, female-only); SPD relevance: high-volume denim and casualwear brand; surface treatment (wash, distress, graphic print) as core design variables; commercial-scale casual print distribution

- lvmh (Manufacturer) — French multinational luxury holding company; formed 1987 (Louis Vuitton + Moët Hennessy merger); ~60 subsidiaries; 75 luxury brands; 6 divisions; HQ Paris 8th arrondissement; Bernard Arnault chairman/CEO since January 1989; 2024 revenue €84.68B; 2025 revenue €80.8B (−4% H1); 215,000 employees; CAC 40; first European company to reach $500B valuation (April 2023); key fashion/leather goods brands: Louis Vuitton (LV monogram canvas pattern; Murakami/Kusama artist collabs), Christian Dior Couture, Fendi (double-F Zucca canvas pattern), Kenzo (bold botanical/floral print identity; H&M collab November 2016), Emilio Pucci (kaleidoscopic multicolour geometric print — most directly print-identified brand in portfolio), Loewe, Givenchy, Marc Jacobs, Loro Piana, Berluti, JW Anderson, Moynat, Patou, Rimowa; key acquisitions: Bulgari $5.2B (2011), Tiffany $16B (January 2021), Barton Perreira eyewear $80M (November 2023); Fenty (Rihanna) launched May 2019 — first new LVMH house in 32 years; Stella McCartney partnership July 2019; Aimé Leon Dore minority stake January 2022; Armani: named preferred buyer up to 54.9% (September 2025, Arnault will); Paris 2024 Olympics €150M premium partner; Formula One $150M/year from 2025; LVMH Young Fashion Designer Prize (from November 2013, Delphine Arnault; €300k + year mentoring; winners: Thomas Tait 2014, Marques'Almeida 2015, Thebe Magugu 2019); Fondation Louis Vuitton (Frank Gehry, Bois de Boulogne, 2014); Journées Particulières biennial open studios (145,000 visitors 2017); selective retail: Le Bon Marché, La Samaritaine, DFS, 24S; media: Les Echos-Le Parisien Group
- macy-s (Retailer) — American department store chain; founded October 28 1858 Manhattan (Rowland Hussey Macy, dry goods); largest US dept store company by retail sales; $25.3B revenue (2023); 94,000 employees; 450 locations (2025); Herald Square flagship 1902 (~1.1M sqft, $3B value, National Historic Landmark); sister brands: Bloomingdale's, Bluemercury; Thanksgiving Day Parade (since 1924); 4th of July Fireworks (since 1976); Straus brothers acquired 1895 (Isidor Straus died Titanic 1912); 1986 LBO; Chapter 11 January 1992; Federated Dept Stores acquisition December 1994; May Dept Stores acquisition February 2005 ($11B); 330+ converted to Macy's 2006; Federated renamed Macy's Inc 2007; Lord & Taylor divested $1.2B (2006); STORY acquired May 2018; Sephora (contracted to Kohl's from 2021); fur ban end fiscal 2020; exotic skins ban February 2023; rejected $5B Arkhouse/Brigade Capital takeover January 2024; $400M Herald Square renovation 2012 (added Gucci, Burberry, Longchamp concessions — documented luxury brands with surface pattern identities entering flagship); employee expense scandal $132–155M hidden (2024); 150 stores closing by end 2026 (announced February 2024); Macy's Backstage (off-price, 319 stores; brands include Chaps/Ralph Lauren, Izod, Arrow); Marshall Field's State Street Chicago: Louis Comfort Tiffany mosaic-capped vaulted ceiling (1910) — applied decorative surface design in retail architecture; first US territory store Puerto Rico 2000; first overseas store Abu Dhabi 2018 (Al Tayer Group)

- liu-jo (Brand) — Italian prêt-à-porter fashion company; founded 1995 Carpi, Modena (Marco "Jo" Marchi + Vannis Marchi); origins in small textile industry; 160 stores Italy + 200 internationally (primarily Europe) as of 2022; name from founders' nicknames; expanded from Liu Jo/Liu Jeans into accessories (jewellery, watches), Baby girls' collection, Underwear and Beachwear, Homewear, shoes (2008); 2012: Milan flagship 350 sqm (L&S group); 2013: Marchon Eyewear licence agreement; 2016–2017: Karlie Kloss, Jourdan Dunn, Martha Hunt, Jasmine Tookes, Joan Smalls campaigns; 2017: corporate reorganisation (Vannis ~10% minority shareholder, Marco controlling); November 2019: Marco Marchi 15% stake in Coin department store chain; November 2019: acquired Blufin (Blugirl + Miss Blumarine brands from Anna Molinari and Gianpaolo Tarabini, Carpi) → "Italian Excellence" multi-brand Italian fashion holding; Anna Molinari retained as ambassador; SPD relevance: underwear/beachwear (print-intensive categories), Blugirl/Miss Blumarine (floral romantic print vocabulary), textile industry origin in Carpi
- longchamp (Brand) — French family-owned leather goods house; founded Paris 1948 (Jean Cassegrain, tobacco/smoking accessories); named after Longchamp Racecourse; jockey logo; world's first luxury leather-covered pipes (1950s); first women's handbag 1971; LM collection 1978 ("created from printed leather"); Le Pliage 1993 (Philippe Cassegrain; foldable nylon canvas + Russia leather handles; trapezoidal; 20M+ units worldwide); currently 3rd generation: Jean Cassegrain (CEO), Sophie Delafontaine (artistic director), Olivier Cassegrain (Director USA); valued $1.5B; 80 countries; 1,500 retail outlets (300 proprietary); 3,200 employees; 20 distribution subsidiaries; six French factories (largest leather manufacture in France; 900 workshop staff); 23,000 sqm logistics centre Segré (2013); Econyl Green District recycled nylon bags (2020); fragrance licence: Interparfums July 2025–2036 (first fragrance 2027); artist collaboration programme (Le Pliage as surface design canvas): Serge Mendjisky (1970s, leather patchwork — among first luxury brands to collaborate with artists), Tracey Emin (2004–2005, personalised luggage/patchwork), Thomas Heatherwick (2004, Zip Bag + NYC SoHo flagship design), Jeremy Scott (2005–2015, 20 styles over 10 years — poodle in space, zodiac signs, credit card, tire tracks, Hollywood postcard), Jean-Luc Moerman (2008, LM reissue illustration), Kate Moss (2010, 12 bags named after London locations + "Kate Moss for Longchamp"), Mary Katrantzou (2011–2012, trompe-l'œil printed totes; orchids/dragons/corals/lanterns), Sarah Morris (2014), Ryan McGinness (2016, "Mindscapes" storefront), Vahram Muratyan (2017), Shayne Oliver/Hood By Air (2018, SS18 capsule), Pokémon (2020, Pikachu jockey motif + Pokémon Go avatar)

- hermes (Brand) — French luxury goods house; founded 1837 Paris (Thierry Hermès); family-controlled (H51 66.72%); 25,000 employees, 7,000 craftsmen; 2024: €15.2B sales, €4.6B net income, 40.5% operating margin; surpassed LVMH as most valuable luxury group April 2025; 16 product divisions: leather goods/saddlery (42.6%), RTW/accessories (29%), silk/textiles (6.3%), watches (3.8%), perfumes (3.5%); core SPD asset: carré de soie silk scarf — first 1937, 3,000+ designs since, 2 collections/year + reprints/editions, 90×90cm, 79g, 300 mulberry moth cocoons per scarf, screen-printed, ~70,000 colour archive, 2020 double-sided printing innovation; HTH facility Pierre-Bénite/Lyon ~800 people; "Brides de Gala" (1957) sold 70,000+ times; Maison d'Hermès: home linens, outdoor textiles, carpets; petit h (2010): offcut creative lab; strict no-licensing policy; Evelyne bag perforated H motif; Chaîne d'ancre motif (1938); Tuareg motifs; WIPO 2021: 7th world industrial design registrations; subsidiaries: John Lobb Bootmaker (1976), Saint-Louis crystal (1989), Puiforcat goldsmith (1993); Grace Wales Bonner: men's RTW creative director, first collection January 2027
- hudson-s-bay (Retailer) — Canadian department store chain; flagship brand of Hudson's Bay Company (HBC), oldest company in North America (founded 1670); first department store 1881 Winnipeg; Harrods-guided full-line model 1913; acquisitions: Morgan's (1960), Freimans (1972), Simpsons (1991), Woodward's (1993); renamed from "the Bay" October 2012, rebrand 6 March 2013 (Lipman design; coat of arms redrawn Mark Summers); NRDC/Saks acquisition July 2008; HBC creditor protection March 2025; all 80 stores closed 1 June 2025; 8,000+ jobs lost; IP acquired by Canadian Tire $30M May 2025 (stripe trademark + coat of arms + Gluckstein/Distinctly Home/Hudson North); core SPD asset: Hudson's Bay Point Blanket stripe — woollen blankets manufactured England from 1779, fur trade currency with First Nations, multi-stripe "Queen Anne colours" pattern, four stripes trademarked worldwide, extended to coats/bags/T-shirts (HBC Signature Shop), sold via Lord & Taylor (US) and Colette (France), became Canadian national symbol, 2010 Winter Olympics campaign; limited-edition Signature collaborations: Virginia Johnson, Pink Tartan, Klaxon Howl; The Room luxury dept (est. 1930s, renovated 2009 Yabu Pushelberg $5.3M 2,000 sq m); designer boutiques: Burberry, Coach, See by Chloé

- christian-louboutin (Brand) — French luxury footwear founded Paris 1991; red outsole (Pantone 18-1663 TPX) as single-colour surface design trademark; ~$300M revenue; Exor 25% stake ($643M, March 2021); nail lacquers 2014; fragrances 2016 (Heatherwick bottles); Vermelho hotel Portugal 2023; Codebox Tile store interiors

## [2026-06-13] enrich | Batch 39: vilebrequin, warnaco-group, wrangler, yves-saint-laurent

Enriched 4 company staging files using Company Editorial Enrichment Mode. Sources: Wikipedia articles appended to each file.

- **Vilebrequin** — Full enrichment. French luxury swimwear brand founded 1971 Saint-Tropez by Fred Prysquel and Yvette; acquired by G-III Apparel Group 2012 for ~$106M. Strong SPD relevance: bold printed swimwear is central to brand identity; art-to-print collaboration with Derrick Adams (2020). enrichment_status → enriched.
- **Warnaco Group** — Full enrichment. Defunct American apparel licensing corporation (founded 1874 as Warner Brothers Corset Manufacturers; renamed 1968; acquired by PVH 2013). Operated Calvin Klein jeans/underwear/swimwear globally, Speedo (US/Canada/Mexico/Caribbean), Warner's, Olga. Historical SPD relevance: large-scale print/branding licensing vehicle; Lycra co-development with DuPont (1959). enrichment_status → enriched.
- **Wrangler** — Minimal enrichment only. Wikipedia source was a disambiguation page with no brand-specific content. Profile notes source limitation; body structured but thin. enrichment_status remains needs_claude; requires Wrangler (jeans) Wikipedia article for full enrichment.
- **Yves Saint Laurent** — Full enrichment. French luxury fashion house (founded 1961/62); part of Kering since 1999; €2.9B revenue 2024; 282 boutiques in 48 countries. Exceptionally strong SPD relevance: Mondrian dress (1965), Hommage à Matisse/Léger/Braque/Van Gogh series; Tom Ford printed silk A/W 2004; Rive Gauche RTW (1966) as first luxury RTW concept. Creative directors listed. enrichment_status → enriched.
